3.396 3.396a.25.25 0 0 0 .354 0l3.396-3.396A.25.25 0 0 0 11.396 7H4.604a.25.25 0 0 0-.177.427Z"></path></svg></span></button></div></nav><div class="d-none"></div></header></div>
</react-partial>


  



<turbo-frame id="repo-content-turbo-frame" target="_top" data-turbo-action="advance" class="">
    <div id="repo-content-pjax-container" class="repository-content " >
    



    
      
  <div class="clearfix container-xl tmp-px-5 tmp-mt-4 page" data-hpc="true" id="wiki-wrapper">
    <div class="d-flex flex-column flex-md-row gh-header">
      <h1 class="flex-auto min-width-0 mb-2 mb-md-0 mr-0 mr-md-2 gh-header-title">Attack Classes &amp; Bypass History</h1>

        <div class="mt-0 mt-lg-1 flex-shrink-0 gh-header-actions">
          <a href="#wiki-pages-box" class="d-md-none ">Jump to bottom</a>

        </div>
    </div>

      <div class="mt-2 mt-md-1 tmp-pb-3 gh-header-meta">
        Cure53 edited this page <relative-time datetime="2026-09-09T08:37:08Z" class="no-wrap">Sep 9, 2026</relative-time>
        &middot;
        <a href="/cure53/DOMPurify/wiki/Attack-Classes-&amp;-Bypass-History/_history" data-view-component="true" class="Link Link--underline">16 revisions</a>
      </div>

    <div id="wiki-content" class="tmp-mt-4">
      <div data-view-component="true" class="Layout Layout--flowRow-until-md Layout--sidebarPosition-end Layout--sidebarPosition-flowRow-end">
  <div data-view-component="true" class="Layout-main">          <div id="wiki-body" class="gollum-markdown-content">
              <div class="markdown-body">
                <div class="markdown-heading"><h1 class="heading-element">Attack Classes &amp; Bypass History</h1><a id="user-content-attack-classes--bypass-history" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Attack Classes &amp; Bypass History" href="#attack-classes--bypass-history"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>This page documents recurring attack classes that DOMPurify and other DOM-based
HTML sanitizers have had to withstand: HTML parser mutation, namespace
confusion, rawtext breakouts, depth-limit flattening, nesting-based mXSS, DOM
clobbering, prototype pollution, template-expression reassembly, engine-deferred
DOM mutation, mixed document contexts on DOM-node input, hook-lifecycle gaps,
and configuration foot-guns.</p>
<p>The examples below are defensive test vectors. They are drawn from DOMPurify's
regression tests, configuration tests, fuzzing work, public advisories, and
historical sanitizer research. Not every example is itself a complete historical
bypass. Some are reduced representatives of a bug class, some pin expected
behavior, and some demonstrate unsafe application use. Every class documented
here is closed in the current release; see
<a href="#status-known-bypasses-against-the-current-release">Status: known bypasses against the current release</a>
for how that was verified and what it does and does not mean.</p>
<p>The purpose of this page is defensive: to help developers understand why these
inputs are dangerous, where they bite, and how to test and configure a sanitizer
so the relevant classes stay closed.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>These are historical or representative classes documented for defensive
testing and education. Treat payloads as regression inputs for your own
pipeline. If you find a working bypass against a supported DOMPurify release,
report it privately through the project's
<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories">security advisories</a>.</p>
</blockquote>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">1. The first rule: sanitized output is context-bound</h2><a id="user-content-1-the-first-rule-sanitized-output-is-context-bound" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 1. The first rule: sanitized output is context-bound" href="#1-the-first-rule-sanitized-output-is-context-bound"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>A sanitizer does not produce safe bytes for every possible sink. It produces
output that is safe only for the parsing context it was designed and tested for.</p>
<p>Typical safe contract:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty);
element.innerHTML = clean;"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">element</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Risky contracts:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="script.text = clean;                 // JavaScript context, not HTML
element.setAttribute('title', clean); // Attribute context, not HTML
svgElement.innerHTML = clean;         // SVG/XML context mismatch
templateEngine.render(clean);         // Second interpreter after HTML
someLibrary.html(clean);              // Library may mutate or reparse"><pre><span class="pl-s1">script</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">text</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>                 <span class="pl-c">// JavaScript context, not HTML</span>
<span class="pl-s1">element</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">setAttribute</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'title'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span> <span class="pl-c">// Attribute context, not HTML</span>
<span class="pl-s1">svgElement</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>         <span class="pl-c">// SVG/XML context mismatch</span>
<span class="pl-s1">templateEngine</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">render</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>         <span class="pl-c">// Second interpreter after HTML</span>
<span class="pl-s1">someLibrary</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">html</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>              <span class="pl-c">// Library may mutate or reparse</span></pre></div>
<p>DOMPurify's own tests check multiple reinsertion paths, including native
<code>innerHTML</code>, jQuery <code>.html()</code>, and <code>document.write()</code> into an iframe. That is not
accidental: XSS bugs often live in the gap between the tree the sanitizer
inspected and the tree the final sink builds. The HTML spec itself
<a href="https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#serialising-html-fragments" rel="nofollow">warns that serialize-then-reparse is not guaranteed to round-trip</a>.</p>
<p><strong>Rule:</strong> sanitize for the exact sink you use, insert without post-processing,
and test the live DOM after insertion, not just the returned string.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">2. Mutation XSS: the browser changes the tree</h2><a id="user-content-2-mutation-xss-the-browser-changes-the-tree" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 2. Mutation XSS: the browser changes the tree" href="#2-mutation-xss-the-browser-changes-the-tree"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Mutation XSS, or mXSS, happens when markup is parsed into one DOM tree during
sanitization but later serializes and reparses into a different, executable tree.
The foundational write-ups for this class against DOMPurify are Michał
Bentkowski's
<a href="https://research.securitum.com/mutation-xss-via-mathml-mutation-dompurify-2-0-17-bypass/" rel="nofollow">namespace-confusion bypass</a>
and Gareth Heyes'
<a href="https://portswigger.net/research/bypassing-dompurify-again-with-mutation-xss" rel="nofollow">follow-up comment-based bypass</a>;
both are worth reading in full.</p>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;style&gt;&lt;a id=&quot;&lt;/style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>&lt;a id<span class="pl-c1">=</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">src</span>=<span class="pl-s">x</span> <span class="pl-c1">onerror</span>=<span class="pl-s">alert(1)</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The sanitizer may inspect a DOM where the dangerous <code>&lt;img&gt;</code> is inert, misplaced,
or hidden inside foreign-content parsing. After serialization and reinsertion,
the browser's HTML parser may repair the markup differently and materialize an
active HTML element.</p>
<p>This is why string comparisons are weak tests:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(payload);

// Weak test: substring checks miss parser mutation and encoding.
console.assert(!clean.includes('onerror'));

// Better test: insert and inspect the resulting DOM.
container.innerHTML = clean;
console.assert(!container.querySelector('[onerror]'));"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">payload</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-c">// Weak test: substring checks miss parser mutation and encoding.</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">includes</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'onerror'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-c">// Better test: insert and inspect the resulting DOM.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'[onerror]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> sanitize, serialize, reparse, and inspect again. That
process must not create new active nodes, executable attributes, unsafe URLs, or
namespace transitions that the sanitizer did not approve.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">3. Namespace confusion: HTML, SVG, and MathML do not parse the same</h2><a id="user-content-3-namespace-confusion-html-svg-and-mathml-do-not-parse-the-same" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 3. Namespace confusion: HTML, SVG, and MathML do not parse the same" href="#3-namespace-confusion-html-svg-and-mathml-do-not-parse-the-same"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>HTML, SVG, and MathML use different parsing and namespace rules. An input can
move between namespaces through foreign-content boundaries, integration points,
and parser error recovery.</p>
<p>Representative payloads:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;math&gt;&lt;mtext&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;mglyph&gt;&lt;style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">mtext</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">table</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">mglyph</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&lt;/style&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>&lt;img src<span class="pl-c1">=</span>x onerr<span class="pl-c1">or</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>alert(1)<span class="pl-c1">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The danger is not that SVG or MathML are inherently unsafe. The danger is that
the same bytes can mean different things depending on whether the current node is
in the HTML, SVG, or MathML namespace. Bentkowski's
<a href="https://research.securitum.com/mutation-xss-via-mathml-mutation-dompurify-2-0-17-bypass/" rel="nofollow">2.0.17 bypass</a>
turned exactly this into XSS by mutating an element's owning namespace across a
serialize/reparse; the fix he proposed - verifying each node against its parent's
namespace - became DOMPurify's long-standing mitigation. A later variant for the
<a href="https://vovohelo.medium.com/from-svg-and-back-yet-another-mutation-xss-via-namespace-confusion-for-dompurify-2-2-2-bypass-5d9ae8b1878f" rel="nofollow">&lt; 2.2.2 bypass</a>
showed the same idea from a different angle.</p>
<p>Applications that only need HTML should reduce attack surface:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, {
  USE_PROFILES: { html: true }
});"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">USE_PROFILES</span>: <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">html</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> every node must be evaluated with its actual namespace,
not just its local tag name.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">4. Integration points: foreign content can re-enter HTML</h2><a id="user-content-4-integration-points-foreign-content-can-re-enter-html" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 4. Integration points: foreign content can re-enter HTML" href="#4-integration-points-foreign-content-can-re-enter-html"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Some SVG and MathML elements are integration points. They sit inside foreign
content but cause descendants to be parsed in an HTML-like way.</p>
<p>Important examples:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;&lt;foreignObject&gt;...&lt;/foreignObject&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">foreignObject</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>...<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">foreignObject</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;math&gt;
  &lt;annotation-xml encoding=&quot;text/html&quot;&gt;...&lt;/annotation-xml&gt;
&lt;/math&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">annotation-xml</span> <span class="pl-c1">encoding</span>="<span class="pl-s">text/html</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>...<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">annotation-xml</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>Payload shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;
  &lt;foreignObject&gt;
    &lt;xmp&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&lt;/xmp&gt;
  &lt;/foreignObject&gt;
&lt;/svg&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">foreignObject</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">xmp</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">src</span>=<span class="pl-s">x</span> <span class="pl-c1">onerror</span>=<span class="pl-s">alert(1)</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">xmp</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">foreignObject</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>A sanitizer must not rely on "we are inside SVG/MathML, therefore descendants are
foreign and inert". Integration points deliberately switch interpretation; the
exact set is defined in the
<a href="https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#html-integration-point" rel="nofollow">HTML tree-construction spec</a>.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> integration-point descendants must be walked and
filtered as active HTML-capable content.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">5. Rawtext and RCDATA breakouts</h2><a id="user-content-5-rawtext-and-rcdata-breakouts" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 5. Rawtext and RCDATA breakouts" href="#5-rawtext-and-rcdata-breakouts"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Several elements switch the tokenizer into a text-like mode.</p>
<p>Important families:</p>
<ul>
<li>RAWTEXT-like: <code>script</code>, <code>style</code>, <code>iframe</code>, <code>xmp</code>, <code>noembed</code>, <code>noframes</code>,
<code>plaintext</code>
</li>
<li>RCDATA-like: <code>textarea</code>, <code>title</code>
</li>
<li>special-case: <code>noscript</code>, whose parsing depends on whether scripting is enabled</li>
</ul>
<p>The RAWTEXT family plus <code>noscript</code> is also the set the HTML fragment serializer
emits <em>literally</em>: their text children are written out unescaped. That matters
for DOM-node input, where a tree the HTML parser could never build (a rawtext
element with an element child, or with its own end tag inside its text) can be
handed to the sanitizer directly; see
<a href="#22-mixed-document-contexts-dom-node-input-from-xmlxhtml-and-the-dom-api">§22</a>.</p>
<p>The dangerous pattern is a closing tag embedded where the sanitizer or a later
wrapper treats it as text, but the final parser treats it as markup.</p>
<p>Examples:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;noscript&gt;&lt;p title=&quot;&lt;/noscript&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">noscript</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span> <span class="pl-c1">title</span>="<span class="pl-s">&lt;/noscript&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;textarea&gt;&lt;p title=&quot;&lt;/textarea&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">textarea</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span> <span class="pl-c1">title</span>="<span class="pl-s">&lt;/textarea&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;style&gt;
  a[href=&quot;&lt;/style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;] {}
&lt;/style&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-ent">a</span>[href<span class="pl-c1">=</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">src</span>=<span class="pl-s">x</span> <span class="pl-c1">onerror</span>=<span class="pl-s">alert(1)</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>"] {}
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>This class is especially sharp when sanitized output is placed into a rawtext or
RCDATA wrapper after sanitization. The sanitizer may have produced safe HTML for
an HTML sink, but the application changed the sink contract by inserting the
result into a text-like parser state. This is the mechanism behind
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-0540" rel="nofollow">CVE-2026-0540</a>
(<a href="https://www.vulncheck.com/advisories/dompurify-xss-via-missing-rawtext-elements-in-safe-for-xml" rel="nofollow">VulnCheck/Fluid Attacks advisory</a>),
where the <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code> attribute regex was missing some rawtext element names
(see <a href="#9-the-safe_for_xml-attribute-regex-the-regex-that-admits-the-boundary">§9</a>).</p>
<p>Server-side parsing adds another wrinkle. In scripting-disabled or server-side
DOM environments, <code>noscript</code> contents may be parsed as HTML rather than inert
text (see <a href="#20-server-side-doms-jsdom-is-part-of-the-tcb">§20</a>).</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> sanitized HTML must not be treated as safe for arbitrary
rawtext or RCDATA wrappers. If an application places sanitized output into such
an element, that is a different sink and needs separate tests.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">6. Depth-limit flattening: when source nesting stops meaning ancestry</h2><a id="user-content-6-depth-limit-flattening-when-source-nesting-stops-meaning-ancestry" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 6. Depth-limit flattening: when source nesting stops meaning ancestry" href="#6-depth-limit-flattening-when-source-nesting-stops-meaning-ancestry"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Deep nesting is not only an availability problem. It can become a security
problem when the parser stops preserving the apparent ancestry from the source
text.</p>
<p>Browsers and DOM implementations have practical nesting limits. Once such a limit
is reached, the parser may continue accepting input, but it no longer keeps
adding descendants below the deepest node. Instead, later nodes are flattened
into sibling positions. WebKit and Blink cap HTML-parser element nesting at
<strong>512</strong> and insert deeper elements as siblings rather than children
(<a href="https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=63082" rel="nofollow">WebKit bug 63082</a>); Gecko
adopted the same "Blink-defined magic depth" for compatibility
(<a href="https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=256180" rel="nofollow">Mozilla bug 256180</a>).
Treat 512 as the well-known historical value, <strong>not</strong> as a portable security
boundary - it is engine- and version-dependent.</p>
<p>Reduced shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;
  &lt;svg&gt;
    &lt;svg&gt;
      &lt;!-- repeated until the parser's nesting behavior changes --&gt;
        &lt;style&gt;
          &lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
      <span class="pl-c">&lt;!-- repeated until the parser's nesting behavior changes --&gt;</span>
        <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
          &lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</pre></div>
<p>The dangerous property is this:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Source nesting != final DOM ancestry"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>Source nesting != final DOM ancestry
</code></pre></div>
<p>This matters especially for sanitizers because they do not sanitize source text.
They sanitize the DOM tree produced by the parser. If extreme nesting causes
flattening before, during, or after sanitizer-relevant tree construction, then
the sanitizer must still make a safe decision about the resulting tree.</p>
<p>A depth-limit regression test should therefore not merely check that
sanitization finishes. It should check that the final inserted DOM is still
inert. Use both a near-threshold value and a deliberately oversized one (for
example 8192) to surface parser, sanitizer, and runtime differences:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="function nest(tag, depth, inner) {
  return `&lt;${tag}&gt;`.repeat(depth) + inner + `&lt;/${tag}&gt;`.repeat(depth);
}

const payload = nest(
  'svg',
  8192,
  '&lt;style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&lt;/style&gt;'
);

const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(payload);

const container = document.createElement('div');
container.innerHTML = clean;

console.assert(!container.querySelector('[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'));
console.assert(!container.querySelector('script'));"><pre><span class="pl-k">function</span> <span class="pl-en">nest</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">depth</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">inner</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s">`&lt;<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">${</span><span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>&gt;`</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">repeat</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">depth</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s1">inner</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s">`&lt;/<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">${</span><span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>&gt;`</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">repeat</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">depth</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">payload</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">nest</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span>
  <span class="pl-s">'svg'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">8192</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-s">'&lt;style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&lt;/style&gt;'</span>
<span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">payload</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">container</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'script'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>A note on environment: very deep trees stress <em>recursive</em> serializers, not just
parsers. Under Node/jsdom (parse5), a tree on the order of 8192 levels can throw
<code>Maximum call stack size exceeded</code> during serialization - a host-side limit, not
a browser result. Pick thresholds that exercise the parser's nesting behavior
rather than your test harness's stack, and remember a green Node run and a green
browser run can mean different things (<a href="#20-server-side-doms-jsdom-is-part-of-the-tcb">§20</a>).</p>
<p>A related, non-mXSS hazard of deep nesting is algorithmic complexity inside
the sanitizer itself. <code>KEEP_CONTENT</code> hoists the children of a removed element
into its parent; up to 3.4.12 the string path did this by deep-cloning the
shrinking subtree at every level, so a chain of nested disallowed elements cost
O(n^2) and pinned whole subtrees in <code>DOMPurify.removed</code>. Since 3.4.13 children
are moved rather than cloned (cloning is reserved for removal of the walk root
itself), which is O(n) and also keeps the in-place guarantee that an original
carrying a queued resource event is relocated and sanitized rather than left
detached but armed. Credit: @n2duc.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> depth-limit behavior must not create active nodes,
executable attributes, unsafe URLs, or namespace surprises in the final inserted
DOM, and must not turn nesting depth into super-linear work. Test both known
engine thresholds and deliberately oversized inputs, with a time bound.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">7. Nesting-based mXSS: mutation chains after flattening and repair</h2><a id="user-content-7-nesting-based-mxss-mutation-chains-after-flattening-and-repair" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 7. Nesting-based mXSS: mutation chains after flattening and repair" href="#7-nesting-based-mxss-mutation-chains-after-flattening-and-repair"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Nesting-based mXSS is the exploit class built on top of depth-limit behavior,
foreign-content parsing, and parser repair. It is distinct from the depth limit
itself, and it is the class behind
<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-gx9m-whjm-85jf">CVE-2024-47875</a>
(reported by <strong>@icesfont</strong>) and the closely related
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2024-45801" rel="nofollow">CVE-2024-45801</a>
(special nesting bypassing the depth check, with prototype pollution able to
weaken it - see <a href="#15-prototype-pollution-as-sanitizer-downgrade">§15</a>).</p>
<p>The relevant pattern is:</p>
<ol>
<li>The browser parses attacker-controlled markup.</li>
<li>Deep or malformed nesting causes flattening or repair.</li>
<li>Some nodes keep surprising namespace or ancestry properties.</li>
<li>DOMPurify walks and sanitizes the DOM it received.</li>
<li>The sanitized DOM is serialized back to HTML.</li>
<li>The application reparses the sanitized string.</li>
<li>The second parse produces a different tree, potentially with active HTML nodes
or executable attributes.</li>
</ol>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;form&gt;
  &lt;math&gt;
    &lt;mtext&gt;
      &lt;table&gt;
        &lt;mglyph&gt;
          &lt;style&gt;
            &lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">form</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">mtext</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
      <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">table</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
        <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">mglyph</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
          <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
            &lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</pre></div>
<p>The interesting part is not the exact tags. The interesting part is the chain of
parser behaviors:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<strong>depth-limit flattening</strong>, where deeply nested descendants are lifted into
shallower positions;</li>
<li>
<strong>namespace-preserving mutation</strong>, where a node is structurally moved but still
carries namespace consequences from where it was first parsed;</li>
<li>
<strong>foreign-content interaction</strong>, where SVG or MathML parsing meets HTML parser
repair;</li>
<li>
<strong>table, caption, and form repair</strong>, where the stack of open elements is
changed in ways that do not match source-text intuition;</li>
<li>
<strong>serialization instability</strong>, where the sanitized DOM stringifies into markup
that parses differently the next time;</li>
<li>
<strong>second-order or third-order mutation</strong>, where parse, sanitize, serialize, and
parse again still does not expose the final tree soon enough.</li>
</ul>
<p>A historically important detail: DOMPurify added an explicit element-nesting
depth cap in 3.1.1 in response to these reports, then <strong>removed it again in
3.1.5</strong>, having concluded that the namespace check plus the <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code>
attribute regex (<a href="#9-the-safe_for_xml-attribute-regex-the-regex-that-admits-the-boundary">§9</a>) already close the class without a numeric cap (documented in
Kevin Mizu's
<a href="https://mizu.re/post/exploring-the-dompurify-library-hunting-for-misconfigurations" rel="nofollow">misconfiguration research</a>).
Current releases therefore rely on those checks rather than a depth counter - a
useful reminder that the defense for this class is <em>parse symmetry plus namespace
integrity</em>, not depth alone.</p>
<p>Bad test:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(payload);
console.assert(!clean.includes('onerror'));"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">payload</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">includes</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'onerror'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Better test:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(payload);

const container = document.createElement('div');
container.innerHTML = clean;

console.assert(!container.querySelector('[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'));
console.assert(!container.querySelector('script'));"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">payload</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">container</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'script'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Better still: test the round trip explicitly.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean1 = DOMPurify.sanitize(payload);

const first = document.createElement('div');
first.innerHTML = clean1;

const serialized = first.innerHTML;

const second = document.createElement('div');
second.innerHTML = serialized;

console.assert(!second.querySelector('[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'));
console.assert(!second.querySelector('script'));"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean1</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">payload</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">first</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">first</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean1</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">serialized</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">first</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">second</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">second</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">serialized</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">second</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">second</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'script'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>For this class, single-pass DOM inspection is not enough. The sanitizer must be
safe across the lifecycle the application actually uses:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="parse -&gt; sanitize -&gt; serialize -&gt; insert -&gt; parse again"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>parse -&gt; sanitize -&gt; serialize -&gt; insert -&gt; parse again
</code></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> sanitized output must remain safe after flattening,
parser repair, serialization, and reparsing. Nesting-based mXSS tests should
combine deep nesting with SVG/MathML, table/caption/form repair, and at least one
full reparse of the sanitized output.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">8. Where DOM-only sanitization is not enough</h2><a id="user-content-8-where-dom-only-sanitization-is-not-enough" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 8. Where DOM-only sanitization is not enough" href="#8-where-dom-only-sanitization-is-not-enough"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>DOMPurify is a DOM-based sanitizer, but some attack classes cannot be fixed by a
DOM walk alone.</p>
<p>That sounds uncomfortable, but it follows from the sanitizer's own trust
boundary:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="source string -&gt; browser parser -&gt; DOM tree -&gt; sanitizer walk -&gt; serialization"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>source string -&gt; browser parser -&gt; DOM tree -&gt; sanitizer walk -&gt; serialization
</code></pre></div>
<p>The sanitizer does not receive the parser's token stream. It receives the DOM
tree after the browser has already applied error recovery, namespace switching,
foster parenting, form repair, depth-limit behavior, and other tree-construction
rules.</p>
<p>For many attacks, that is exactly what we want: sanitize the tree the browser
actually built. But for some mXSS classes, the DOM is already too late. The
security-relevant fact may have existed only in the source string or in parser
state that the DOM API does not expose.</p>
<p>Examples:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<strong>Depth-limit flattening:</strong> after extreme nesting, the final DOM no longer
proves how deeply nested the source text was.</li>
<li>
<strong>Multi-parse mXSS:</strong> the first DOM tree can serialize into a string that
reparses into a different tree.</li>
<li>
<strong>Rawtext/RCDATA breakouts:</strong> dangerous closing tags can be hidden inside
attribute values until the output is placed into a different text-like parser
state.</li>
<li>
<strong>Comment and attribute smuggling:</strong> markup-like byte sequences inside
attributes can become meaningful only after serialization and another parse.</li>
</ul>
<p>This is where narrow string-level guards are legitimate. They are not a
replacement for DOM sanitization, and they are not an attempt to parse HTML with
regular expressions. They are pre-parser or pre-serializer tripwires for patterns
that the DOM cannot faithfully represent after parsing. DOMPurify's own history
makes the point concrete: when it removed the numeric depth cap in 3.1.5 (<a href="#7-nesting-based-mxss-mutation-chains-after-flattening-and-repair">§7</a>), it
leaned on exactly such a lexical guard - the <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code> attribute regex (<a href="#9-the-safe_for_xml-attribute-regex-the-regex-that-admits-the-boundary">§9</a>) -
to keep the relevant classes closed.</p>
<p>In other words:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOM walk: remove dangerous nodes and attributes from the tree.
Lexical guard: reject or neutralize source shapes that make the tree unstable."><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>DOM walk: remove dangerous nodes and attributes from the tree.
Lexical guard: reject or neutralize source shapes that make the tree unstable.
</code></pre></div>
<p>This is the sad but honest lesson:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Do not use regex to sanitize HTML.
Do use narrow lexical checks to reject inputs whose parser state cannot be
safely recovered from the DOM."><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>Do not use regex to sanitize HTML.
Do use narrow lexical checks to reject inputs whose parser state cannot be
safely recovered from the DOM.
</code></pre></div>
<p>There is no general HTML sanitizer regex. But there may be one very specific
regex to rule one very specific parser-mutation class.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> DOM sanitization must be complemented by narrowly scoped
source-level or attribute-value guards for parser-state hazards that are lost
after parsing. Those guards should be simple, auditable, fail-closed, and covered
by regression tests at known parser-depth and multi-parse thresholds.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">9. The SAFE_FOR_XML attribute regex: the regex that admits the boundary</h2><a id="user-content-9-the-safe_for_xml-attribute-regex-the-regex-that-admits-the-boundary" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 9. The SAFE_FOR_XML attribute regex: the regex that admits the boundary" href="#9-the-safe_for_xml-attribute-regex-the-regex-that-admits-the-boundary"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>DOMPurify's <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code> handling contains an intentionally narrow regex in
attribute sanitization. At the time of writing, the relevant check in
<code>purify.ts</code> looks like this:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-ts notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="/* Work around a security issue with comments inside attributes */
if (
  SAFE_FOR_XML &amp;&amp;
  regExpTest(
    /((--!?|])&gt;)|&lt;\/(style|script|title|xmp|textarea|noscript|iframe|noembed|noframes)/i,
    value
  )
) {
  _removeAttribute(name, currentNode);
  continue;
}"><pre><span class="pl-c">/* Work around a security issue with comments inside attributes */</span>
<span class="pl-k">if</span> <span class="pl-kos">(</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">SAFE_FOR_XML</span> <span class="pl-c1">&amp;&amp;</span>
  <span class="pl-en">regExpTest</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span>
    <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">-</span><span class="pl-s">-</span><span class="pl-s">!</span><span class="pl-c1">?</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-kos">]</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-s">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">&lt;</span><span class="pl-cce">\/</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">s</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-s">y</span><span class="pl-s">l</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">s</span><span class="pl-s">c</span><span class="pl-s">r</span><span class="pl-s">i</span><span class="pl-s">p</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-s">i</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-s">l</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">x</span><span class="pl-s">m</span><span class="pl-s">p</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">x</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-s">a</span><span class="pl-s">r</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">a</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">n</span><span class="pl-s">o</span><span class="pl-s">s</span><span class="pl-s">c</span><span class="pl-s">r</span><span class="pl-s">i</span><span class="pl-s">p</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">i</span><span class="pl-s">f</span><span class="pl-s">r</span><span class="pl-s">a</span><span class="pl-s">m</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">n</span><span class="pl-s">o</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">m</span><span class="pl-s">b</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">d</span><span class="pl-c1">|</span><span class="pl-s">n</span><span class="pl-s">o</span><span class="pl-s">f</span><span class="pl-s">r</span><span class="pl-s">a</span><span class="pl-s">m</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">s</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span>i</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">value</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">)</span>
<span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-en">_removeAttribute</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">currentNode</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-k">continue</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p>This is not DOMPurify trying to sanitize HTML with a regex. It is an
attribute-value tripwire for sequences that are dangerous precisely because the
DOM abstraction is no longer enough. It exists because of two concrete bypasses:
Gareth Heyes' <a href="https://portswigger.net/research/bypassing-dompurify-again-with-mutation-xss" rel="nofollow">comment-in-attribute mXSS</a>
(the <code>(--!?|])&gt;</code> half) and the missing-rawtext-element advisory
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-0540" rel="nofollow">CVE-2026-0540</a> (which extended
the element list in the second half).</p>
<p>The regex catches two broad families:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="((--!?|])&gt;)"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>((--!?|])&gt;)
</code></pre></div>
<p>This catches comment / declaration / CDATA-ish closers such as <code>--&gt;</code>, <code>--!&gt;</code>, and
<code>]&gt;</code>.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;\/(style|script|title|xmp|textarea|noscript|iframe|noembed|noframes)"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>&lt;\/(style|script|title|xmp|textarea|noscript|iframe|noembed|noframes)
</code></pre></div>
<p>This catches rawtext and RCDATA closing tags inside attribute values.</p>
<p>Those strings are dangerous in attribute values because a later serialization and
reparse can move them from "just attribute text" into "parser control syntax". In
other words, they are not dangerous because the current DOM attribute executes.
They are dangerous because they can become syntax in a later parser state.</p>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;noscript&gt;&lt;p title=&quot;&lt;/noscript&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">noscript</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span> <span class="pl-c1">title</span>="<span class="pl-s">&lt;/noscript&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>At the moment the sanitizer sees an attribute, <code>&lt;/noscript&gt;</code> is only text. But if
sanitized output is later inserted into a <code>noscript</code> context, the same bytes can
terminate that context and let the following <code>&lt;img&gt;</code> parse as markup. The same
idea applies to other rawtext and RCDATA wrappers:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;textarea&gt;&lt;p title=&quot;&lt;/textarea&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">textarea</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">p</span> <span class="pl-c1">title</span>="<span class="pl-s">&lt;/textarea&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;style&gt;
  a[href=&quot;&lt;/style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;&quot;] {}
&lt;/style&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-ent">a</span>[href<span class="pl-c1">=</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">src</span>=<span class="pl-s">x</span> <span class="pl-c1">onerror</span>=<span class="pl-s">alert(1)</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>"] {}
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The regex therefore encodes a pragmatic boundary:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="If an attribute value contains parser-control syntax that can break out of a
later text-like context, remove the attribute."><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>If an attribute value contains parser-control syntax that can break out of a
later text-like context, remove the attribute.
</code></pre></div>
<p>That is not elegant, but it is honest. The DOM cannot tell us enough about all
future parser states. A small lexical guard is the right tool for this specific
edge.</p>
<p>Security consequences:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<code>SAFE_FOR_XML: true</code> keeps this guard enabled.</li>
<li>
<code>SAFE_FOR_XML: false</code> disables this family of protection.</li>
<li>Disabling it is only reasonable for tightly constrained HTML-only use where
SVG, MathML, XML-like parsing, and rawtext/RCDATA reinsertion hazards are out
of scope.</li>
<li>If sanitized output is ever reinserted into <code>style</code>, <code>title</code>, <code>textarea</code>,
<code>xmp</code>, <code>noscript</code>, <code>iframe</code>, <code>noembed</code>, or <code>noframes</code>-like contexts, this guard
matters.</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> attribute values must not be allowed to carry
parser-control syntax that can become active only after serialization and
reinsertion. This is a legitimate lexical check, not a general regex-based
sanitizer.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">10. Foster parenting and parser repair</h2><a id="user-content-10-foster-parenting-and-parser-repair" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 10. Foster parenting and parser repair" href="#10-foster-parenting-and-parser-repair"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Foster parenting is a specific HTML parser repair rule, mostly relevant around
tables. It overlaps with nesting-based mXSS, but it should not be merged with
depth-limit flattening.</p>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;table&gt;&lt;script&gt;alert(1)&lt;/script&gt;&lt;/table&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">table</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">script</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-en">alert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">script</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">table</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The parser may move misplaced content outside the table structure
(<a href="https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/parsing.html#foster-parent" rel="nofollow">HTML spec: foster parenting</a>).
A DOM-based sanitizer sees the repaired tree, not the literal source string. That
is usually a strength, but bugs appear when sanitizer logic assumes ancestry,
ownership, or context based on where markup appeared in the original string.</p>
<p>This is related to nesting-based mXSS because both involve parser repair, but the
mechanisms are different:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Depth-limit flattening: too much nesting changes ancestry.
Nesting-based mXSS: flattening/repair plus serialization creates a new tree.
Foster parenting: table insertion rules relocate misplaced nodes."><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>Depth-limit flattening: too much nesting changes ancestry.
Nesting-based mXSS: flattening/repair plus serialization creates a new tree.
Foster parenting: table insertion rules relocate misplaced nodes.
</code></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> sanitizer traversal must operate on the parser-produced
tree, not on source-text intuition. Misnested table, form, SVG, and MathML
content must be tested after insertion into the real sink.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">11. DOM clobbering: markup changes object lookups</h2><a id="user-content-11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 11. DOM clobbering: markup changes object lookups" href="#11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>DOM clobbering abuses named elements to shadow properties on <code>document</code>,
<code>window</code>, forms, or other host objects - no script required. For background, see
PortSwigger's
<a href="https://portswigger.net/research/dom-clobbering-strikes-back" rel="nofollow">DOM clobbering strikes back</a>.</p>
<p>Classic primitive:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;form&gt;
  &lt;input name=nodeName&gt;
&lt;/form&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">form</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">input</span> <span class="pl-c1">name</span>=<span class="pl-s">nodeName</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">form</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>If sanitizer internals read security-sensitive properties through instance
lookups, attacker-created nodes can interfere with assumptions:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="node.nodeName
node.parentNode
node.attributes
node.removeChild"><pre><span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">nodeName</span>
<span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">parentNode</span>
<span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">attributes</span>
<span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">removeChild</span></pre></div>
<p>Two bounding observations:</p>
<ul>
<li>A clobbered property is usually a node reference, not an arbitrary string, so
the technique cannot make a plain <code>&lt;a&gt;</code> report a fake tag name; but it can
still confuse logic, cause exceptions, or skip cleanup. The override only
applies to elements that expose named children (forms via
<code>LegacyOverrideBuiltIns</code>, plus <code>document</code>/<code>window</code>).</li>
<li>A clobbered <code>form</code> can also be reached via an external <code>form=</code> association (an
input elsewhere in the document pointing at the form by <code>id</code>), which the
sanitizer must account for when deciding whether a node is clobbered.</li>
</ul>
<p>A subtle internal trap: any node member the sanitizer reads or calls must
either go through a cached prototype accessor or be one the clobbering check
actually covers. DOMPurify's <code>_isClobbered</code> (as of 3.4.15) probes <code>nodeName</code>,
<code>textContent</code>, <code>removeChild</code>, <code>attributes</code>, <code>removeAttribute</code>,
<code>removeAttributeNode</code>, <code>getAttributeNode</code>, <code>setAttribute</code>, <code>namespaceURI</code>,
<code>insertBefore</code>, <code>hasChildNodes</code>, <code>nodeType</code>, and <code>childNodes</code> - so reaching
for a member outside that set (for example <code>getAttributeNames</code>) on a
distrusted node reintroduces the gap. Two recent examples of the gap
re-opening:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<code>ownerDocument</code> (fixed in 3.4.13): <code>_createNodeIterator</code> read
<code>root.ownerDocument</code> directly. On an <code>IN_PLACE</code> form root with
<code>&lt;input name="ownerDocument"&gt;</code> that read returns the input,
<code>createNodeIterator.call(&lt;input&gt;, …)</code> throws "Illegal invocation", and the
throw landed <em>before</em> the walk's fail-closed barrier, so the caller's live
tree kept its armed handlers. The read now goes through the cached
<code>Node.prototype.ownerDocument</code> getter and the iterator is constructed inside
the barrier.</li>
<li>
<code>removeAttributeNode</code> / <code>getAttributeNode</code> (fixed in 3.4.15): the exact-Attr
removal introduced for case-preserved attribute names
(<a href="#22-mixed-document-contexts-dom-node-input-from-xmlxhtml-and-the-dom-api">§22</a>)
reached for <code>element.removeAttributeNode(attr)</code>. A form child of that name
shadows the method, the call throws, and the name-based fallback lowercases
its lookup key and misses <code>ONANIMATIONSTART</code>. The method is now called
through the cached <code>Element.prototype</code> reference and both names are in the
clobbering probe, so such a form is removed wholesale.</li>
</ul>
<p>The corollary: when a node selected for removal cannot be detached, <strong>fail
closed</strong> rather than calling its own (clobberable) methods to "neutralize" it.</p>
<p>DOMPurify offers controls for this family:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, { SANITIZE_DOM: true });        // default-on
DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, { SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS: true }); // prefix id/name"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">SANITIZE_DOM</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>        <span class="pl-c">// default-on</span>
<span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span> <span class="pl-c">// prefix id/name</span></pre></div>
<p><code>SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS</code> rewrites user-supplied <code>id</code>/<code>name</code> into a safer
<code>user-content-*</code> form.</p>
<p>A testing caveat: jsdom does <strong>not</strong> implement <code>HTMLFormElement</code>'s
<code>LegacyOverrideBuiltIns</code>, so form-based clobbering of built-ins does not occur
under jsdom at all - clobbering regressions only reproduce in a real browser.
Run clobbering corpora in-browser and have them self-report whether the override
is live in the current engine, so a green Node run is not mistaken for coverage.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> sanitizer internals must use cached, realm-safe
prototype accessors for security-critical DOM properties and must not trust named
properties on live instances.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">12. Cross-realm DOM input and IN_PLACE</h2><a id="user-content-12-cross-realm-dom-input-and-in_place" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 12. Cross-realm DOM input and IN_PLACE" href="#12-cross-realm-dom-input-and-in_place"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>String input and DOM-node input have different risk profiles.</p>
<p>With string input, DOMPurify controls parsing:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty);"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>With DOM input, the caller supplies live nodes:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(node, { IN_PLACE: true });"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">IN_PLACE</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Those nodes may come from another realm, such as an iframe, where constructors
and prototypes differ:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const iframeNode = iframe.contentDocument.createElement('a');
iframeNode.href = 'javascript:alert(1)';

DOMPurify.sanitize(iframeNode, { IN_PLACE: true });"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">iframeNode</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">iframe</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">contentDocument</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'a'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">iframeNode</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">href</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">'javascript:alert(1)'</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">iframeNode</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">IN_PLACE</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Naive checks such as <code>node instanceof Element</code> can fail across realms. Live nodes
may also contain shadow roots, clobbered names, or unexpected getters installed
by hostile application code. A clobbered root that cannot be safely classified
should be rejected outright rather than processed
(<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-r47g-fvhr-h676">GHSA-r47g-fvhr-h676</a>
is the clobbered-<code>form</code>-root example of this).</p>
<p>Rejecting is not enough on its own, because the rejected tree is <em>live</em>: the
caller built it in the page, so an <code>&lt;img onerror&gt;</code> inside it may already have a
queued error event. "Throw and leave the tree as it was" therefore hands back an
armed subtree. The 3.4.12-3.4.15 hardening run closed the remaining edges of
that contract:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<strong>Fail closed before every throw.</strong> A forbidden or clobbered root is
neutralized (every non-allow-listed attribute stripped from the whole
subtree, then the root emptied) <em>before</em> the <code>TypeError</code> is thrown; a throw
mid-walk neutralizes the root and every subtree already detached during that
