walk. Attribute stripping runs before detaching, so a detached original
cannot keep a handler that fires in page scope.</li>
<li>
<strong>Patch-linkage pre-pass.</strong> Before the walk starts, the whole in-place tree is
swept once and every declarative-partial-updates linkage (<code>patchsrc</code>, <code>for</code>
off <code>&lt;label&gt;</code>/<code>&lt;output&gt;</code>, processing-instruction and markup-bearing comment
range markers) is severed, so a patch cannot fire as a microtask mid-walk and
inject into a region the iterator already passed (see
<a href="#19-declarative-partial-updates-chrome-150">§19</a>). Gated on <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code>.</li>
<li>
<strong>Removal bookkeeping is part of the security contract.</strong> The post-walk
<code>IN_PLACE</code> pass neutralizes every subtree recorded in <code>DOMPurify.removed</code>. A
value write-back (trim, template scrubbing, Trusted Types) used to pop the
last <code>removed</code> entry unconditionally, which could consume an unrelated
subtree record and let that detached subtree keep a live handler; the pop is
now tied to the one path that owns a record (the <code>SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS</code>
remove-and-recreate). Fixed in 3.4.15.</li>
<li>
<strong>Fragment input with shadow hosts.</strong> When the input is a <code>DocumentFragment</code>,
appending it into the working body empties the fragment; the attached-shadow
traversal must therefore start from the body, not from the (now empty)
fragment, or a child host's shadow root is never inspected. Fixed in 3.4.15
(reproduces only in engines with clonable shadow roots).</li>
<li>
<strong>Clobber-safe reads everywhere on the path</strong>, including <code>ownerDocument</code>
(<a href="#11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups">§11</a>).</li>
</ul>
<p>One caveat is irreducible: DOMPurify cannot undo an engine mutation that fired
<em>before</em> <code>sanitize()</code> was called. Sanitize an attacker-controlled tree before it
is connected to the live document, not after.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> classify nodes by DOM capability and safe accessors, not
by same-realm constructors. Walk attached shadow roots (also for fragment
input), reject disallowed or clobbered root nodes, and neutralize before every
throw so a rejected live tree is never handed back armed.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">13. Template-expression reassembly: SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES</h2><a id="user-content-13-template-expression-reassembly-safe_for_templates" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 13. Template-expression reassembly: SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES" href="#13-template-expression-reassembly-safe_for_templates"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p><code>SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES</code> is meant to strip template syntax such as:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="{{ ... }}
${ ... }
&lt;% ... %&gt;"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>{{ ... }}
${ ... }
&lt;% ... %&gt;
</code></pre></div>
<p>This mode exists for applications that feed sanitized HTML into a client-side
template engine, but it should be treated as a last resort. The safer design is
to avoid passing user-controlled HTML through a second template interpreter at
all.</p>
<p>The subtle bug class is that a template expression can be split across multiple
text nodes, then reassembled after disallowed elements are removed or after
<code>normalize()</code> merges adjacent text nodes.</p>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;div id=app&gt;{&lt;foo&gt;&lt;/foo&gt;{constructor.constructor(&quot;alert(1)&quot;)()}&lt;foo&gt;&lt;/foo&gt;}&lt;/div&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">div</span> <span class="pl-c1">id</span>=<span class="pl-s">app</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>{<span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">foo</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">foo</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>{constructor.constructor("alert(1)")()}<span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">foo</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">foo</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>}<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">div</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>Before removal, no single text node contains a complete expression. After <code>&lt;foo&gt;</code>
is removed and adjacent text nodes merge, they join into:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="{{constructor.constructor(&quot;alert(1)&quot;)()}}"><pre>{{constructor.constructor("alert(1)")()}}</pre></div>
<p>Two related pitfalls:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<strong>Return mode:</strong> scrubbing that only runs on the final serialized string can
miss <code>RETURN_DOM</code>, <code>RETURN_DOM_FRAGMENT</code>, or <code>IN_PLACE</code> flows.</li>
<li>
<strong>The expression regexes themselves:</strong> an over-narrow template-literal pattern
was itself a bypass -
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-26791" rel="nofollow">CVE-2025-26791</a> (incorrect
<code>${ ... }</code> handling under <code>SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES</code>, fixed in 3.2.4).</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> template scrubbing must happen after node removal and
text-node merging for every return path: string, DOM, fragment, and in-place.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">14. Custom elements and permissive configuration</h2><a id="user-content-14-custom-elements-and-permissive-configuration" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 14. Custom elements and permissive configuration" href="#14-custom-elements-and-permissive-configuration"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>By default, unknown custom elements should not be allowed. DOMPurify's
<code>CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING</code> option is deliberately restrictive unless the
application opts in with <code>tagNameCheck</code>, <code>attributeNameCheck</code>, and optional
customized built-in handling.</p>
<p>Risky shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, {
  CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING: {
    tagNameCheck: /.*/,
    attributeNameCheck: /.*/,
    allowCustomizedBuiltInElements: true
  }
});"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING</span>: <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">tagNameCheck</span>: <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span>.<span class="pl-c1">*</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span></span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">attributeNameCheck</span>: <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span>.<span class="pl-c1">*</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span></span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">allowCustomizedBuiltInElements</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>That kind of configuration turns custom elements into a broad escape hatch. Even
if the custom element itself is inert, arbitrary attributes, lifecycle behavior,
framework hydration, or later application logic may make it dangerous.</p>
<p>Safer shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, {
  CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING: {
    tagNameCheck: /^my-widget$/,
    attributeNameCheck: (attr, tag) =&gt;
      tag === 'my-widget' &amp;&amp; ['data-id', 'aria-label'].includes(attr),
    allowCustomizedBuiltInElements: false
  }
});"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING</span>: <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">tagNameCheck</span>: <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span><span class="pl-cce">^</span><span class="pl-s">m</span><span class="pl-s">y</span><span class="pl-s">-</span><span class="pl-s">w</span><span class="pl-s">i</span><span class="pl-s">d</span><span class="pl-s">g</span><span class="pl-s">e</span><span class="pl-s">t</span><span class="pl-cce">$</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span></span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-en">attributeNameCheck</span>: <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">attr</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span>
      <span class="pl-s1">tag</span> <span class="pl-c1">===</span> <span class="pl-s">'my-widget'</span> <span class="pl-c1">&amp;&amp;</span> <span class="pl-kos">[</span><span class="pl-s">'data-id'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s">'aria-label'</span><span class="pl-kos">]</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">includes</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">attr</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">allowCustomizedBuiltInElements</span>: <span class="pl-c1">false</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> custom-element allow-lists should be narrow,
tag-specific, and must not bypass URI, event-handler, namespace, or
forbidden-tag checks.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">15. Prototype pollution as sanitizer downgrade</h2><a id="user-content-15-prototype-pollution-as-sanitizer-downgrade" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 15. Prototype pollution as sanitizer downgrade" href="#15-prototype-pollution-as-sanitizer-downgrade"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>A sanitizer must assume the surrounding JavaScript environment may already be
compromised by prototype pollution. Pollution can turn an application bug
elsewhere into a sanitizer downgrade if internal config objects inherit
attacker-controlled properties.</p>
<p>Representative shape:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="// Pollution happens elsewhere in the application.
Object.prototype.tagNameCheck = /.*/;
Object.prototype.attributeNameCheck = /.*/;

// Later, with default config:
const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize('&lt;x-x autofocus tabindex=0 onfocus=alert(1)&gt;');"><pre><span class="pl-c">// Pollution happens elsewhere in the application.</span>
<span class="pl-v">Object</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">prototype</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">tagNameCheck</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span>.<span class="pl-c1">*</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span></span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-v">Object</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">prototype</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">attributeNameCheck</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span>.<span class="pl-c1">*</span><span class="pl-c1">/</span></span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-c">// Later, with default config:</span>
<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'&lt;x-x autofocus tabindex=0 onfocus=alert(1)&gt;'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>This is not hypothetical. It is essentially
<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-v9jr-rg53-9pgp">CVE-2026-41238</a>:
in 3.0.1-3.3.3, a <code>|| {}</code> fallback in the config parser inherited from
<code>Object.prototype</code>, so a prior PP gadget that set <code>tagNameCheck</code>/
<code>attributeNameCheck</code> on <code>Object.prototype</code> made DOMPurify admit arbitrary custom
elements with event handlers under the <strong>default</strong> configuration (fixed in 3.4.0
by initializing prototype-free). 3.0.0 and 2.x were unaffected because they used
<code>Object.create(null)</code>.</p>
<p>Related prototype-pollution classes in DOMPurify's history:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2024-45801" rel="nofollow">CVE-2024-45801</a> - PP used to
weaken the (then-present) nesting depth check (<a href="#7-nesting-based-mxss-mutation-chains-after-flattening-and-repair">§7</a>).</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-cj63-jhhr-wcxv">GHSA-cj63-jhhr-wcxv</a> -
<code>USE_PROFILES</code> <code>Array.prototype</code> pollution (fixed in 3.3.2). The array side of
the chain matters too.</li>
</ul>
<p>DOMPurify's structural defenses (carried by current releases): internal config is
created prototype-free, the incoming config is cloned before use, and presence is
tested with own-property checks rather than inherited reads.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> internal config objects must use null prototypes or
own-property checks. Security decisions must never read inherited properties from
attacker-controllable prototypes. Because PP gadgets are common in the ecosystem
(lodash, jQuery.extend, qs, merge-deep, …), treat "an attacker can pollute
<code>Object.prototype</code>" as a realistic precondition.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">16. Allow-list and block-list precedence</h2><a id="user-content-16-allow-list-and-block-list-precedence" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 16. Allow-list and block-list precedence" href="#16-allow-list-and-block-list-precedence"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Configuration flags are part of the attack surface. A particularly sharp class is
predicate-based allow-listing:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize('&lt;iframe src=&quot;https://evil.example&quot;&gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;', {
  ADD_TAGS: () =&gt; true,
  FORBID_TAGS: ['iframe']
});"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'&lt;iframe src="https://evil.example"&gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-en">ADD_TAGS</span>: <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-c1">true</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">FORBID_TAGS</span>: <span class="pl-kos">[</span><span class="pl-s">'iframe'</span><span class="pl-kos">]</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>The defensive rule is simple:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="FORBID_* must always win over ADD_*."><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>FORBID_* must always win over ADD_*.
</code></pre></div>
<p>This applies even when <code>ADD_TAGS</code> or <code>ADD_ATTR</code> are functions.</p>
<p>Bad design:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="if (config.ADD_TAGS(tagName)) {
  allowNode(node);
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">if</span> <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">config</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">ADD_TAGS</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">tagName</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-en">allowNode</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p>Better design:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="if (isForbidden(tagName)) {
  removeNode(node);
} else if (isDefaultAllowed(tagName) || isExplicitlyAdded(tagName)) {
  allowNode(node);
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">if</span> <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-en">isForbidden</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">tagName</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-en">removeNode</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span> <span class="pl-k">else</span> <span class="pl-k">if</span> <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-en">isDefaultAllowed</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">tagName</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-c1">||</span> <span class="pl-en">isExplicitlyAdded</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">tagName</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-en">allowNode</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">node</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> user-supplied predicates may add only after all hard
block-lists, namespace checks, URI checks, and event-handler checks have run.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">17. URI scheme confusion</h2><a id="user-content-17-uri-scheme-confusion" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 17. URI scheme confusion" href="#17-uri-scheme-confusion"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>URI-bearing attributes are dangerous because execution may be hidden in a value,
not in a tag name.</p>
<p>Examples:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;a href=&quot;javascript:alert(1)&quot;&gt;click&lt;/a&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">a</span> <span class="pl-c1">href</span>="<span class="pl-s">javascript:alert(1)</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>click<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">a</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;math&gt;&lt;mi xlink:href=&quot;data:x,&lt;script&gt;alert(1)&lt;/script&gt;&quot;&gt;&lt;/mi&gt;&lt;/math&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">mi</span> <span class="pl-c1">xlink:href</span>="<span class="pl-s">data:x,&lt;script&gt;alert(1)&lt;/script&gt;</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">mi</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">math</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>Sensitive URI-related configuration includes:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS
ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR
ALLOWED_URI_REGEXP
ADD_DATA_URI_TAGS"><pre><span class="pl-c1">ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS</span>
<span class="pl-c1">ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR</span>
<span class="pl-c1">ALLOWED_URI_REGEXP</span>
<span class="pl-c1">ADD_DATA_URI_TAGS</span></pre></div>
<p>Risky shapes:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, { ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS: true });
DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty, { ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR: ['data-target'] });"><pre><span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS</span>: <span class="pl-c1">true</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-c1">ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR</span>: <span class="pl-kos">[</span><span class="pl-s">'data-target'</span><span class="pl-kos">]</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>If an application later reads <code>data-target</code> as a URL and navigates to it, the
sanitizer cannot know that this custom attribute has become a URL sink.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> adding an attribute to an allow-list must not skip URI
validation. URI checks must run after entity, whitespace, control-character, and
template normalization.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">18. Engine-deferred mutation: selectedcontent</h2><a id="user-content-18-engine-deferred-mutation-selectedcontent" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 18. Engine-deferred mutation: selectedcontent" href="#18-engine-deferred-mutation-selectedcontent"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Some browser features create or refresh DOM subtrees after the sanitizer has
already walked them. The <code>&lt;selectedcontent&gt;</code> element is a clean example:
<a href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-47423" rel="nofollow">CVE-2026-47423</a> /
GHSA-87xg-pxx2-7hvx, where DOMPurify 3.4.4 allowed <code>&lt;selectedcontent&gt;</code> by default
and Chrome (130+) "re-clones" the selected <code>&lt;option&gt;</code>'s subtree into it <em>after</em>
sanitization (fixed in 3.4.5 by forbidding it unless explicitly opted in).</p>
<p>Published vector:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;select&gt;
  &lt;button&gt;&lt;selectedcontent&gt;&lt;/selectedcontent&gt;&lt;/button&gt;
  &lt;option selected=javascript:1&gt;
    &lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;x
  &lt;/option&gt;
&lt;/select&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">select</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">button</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">selectedcontent</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">selectedcontent</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">button</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">option</span> <span class="pl-c1">selected</span>=<span class="pl-s">javascript:1</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">src</span>=<span class="pl-s">x</span> <span class="pl-c1">onerror</span>=<span class="pl-s">alert(1)</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>x
  <span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">option</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">select</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p>The execution chain is the important part:</p>
<ol>
<li>The browser builds an initial <code>&lt;selectedcontent&gt;</code> clone from the selected
<code>&lt;option&gt;</code>.</li>
<li>DOMPurify walks the tree and sanitizes that clone, and removes
<code>selected=javascript:1</code> from the original <code>&lt;option&gt;</code> (a normal step).</li>
<li>
<strong>After</strong> the walk, the engine refreshes the <code>&lt;selectedcontent&gt;</code> clone from
the original option subtree - which still contains <code>&lt;img src=x onerror=…&gt;</code>.</li>
<li>The refreshed clone lands in a subtree DOMPurify already visited, and is never
re-inspected.</li>
</ol>
<p>So the danger is not the <code>selected=javascript:1</code> attribute (which is removed and
is largely a red herring) - it is the <strong>post-walk re-clone</strong> of the option's
content. This is a general lesson: any element that clones, projects, hydrates,
imports, or lazily populates content must either be forbidden by default or be
followed by a second inspection after the engine settles.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> re-walk subtrees that the engine clones or defers
("refresh after sanitize"), or forbid such elements by default.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">19. Declarative partial updates (Chrome 150+)</h2><a id="user-content-19-declarative-partial-updates-chrome-150" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 19. Declarative partial updates (Chrome 150+)" href="#19-declarative-partial-updates-chrome-150"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>A newer member of a similar class as above arrives with
<a href="https://github.com/WICG/declarative-partial-updates">declarative partial updates</a>
(shipping in Chromium-based browsers around Chrome 150). A <code>&lt;template for="…"&gt;</code>
does not render in place: on connection/stream - <em>after</em> a parse-time sanitizer
has finished - the engine <strong>teleports</strong> the template's content into a
pre-existing element chosen by id or by a <code>&lt;?…?&gt;</code> processing-instruction marker,
optionally deleting a range of existing nodes. A <code>patchsrc</code> attribute fetches
remote markup and applies it the same way (treated as script-loading for CSP).</p>
<p>Why this <em>could</em> defeat a parse-time sanitizer is structural, not a parsing
quirk: if the patch fires against the <em>live</em> tree after the walk, it moves
already-walked content into a context the walk never saw. It is the
selectedcontent lesson again (mutation after the walk) with the added twist
that the destination can be a <strong>foreign-content</strong> node, which turns it into a
namespace-confusion mXSS delivery vehicle (cf.
<a href="#3-namespace-confusion-html-svg-and-mathml-do-not-parse-the-same">§3</a>,
<a href="#4-integration-points-foreign-content-can-re-enter-html">§4</a>,
<a href="#5-rawtext-and-rcdata-breakouts">§5</a>):</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-text-html-basic notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;svg&gt;&lt;?marker name=&quot;x&quot;&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;
&lt;template for=&quot;x&quot;&gt;&lt;style&gt;&lt;img src onerror=alert()&gt;&lt;/style&gt;&lt;/template&gt;"><pre><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span>?marker name="x"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">svg</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">template</span> <span class="pl-c1">for</span>="<span class="pl-s">x</span>"<span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span>&lt;img src onerr<span class="pl-c1">or</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>alert()<span class="pl-c1">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">style</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span><span class="pl-kos">&lt;/</span><span class="pl-ent">template</span><span class="pl-kos">&gt;</span></pre></div>
<p><code>&lt;style&gt;&lt;img …&gt;</code> is inert rawtext <em>inside the template</em>, but teleporting it into
the SVG marker and round-tripping through serialize/reparse shifts the <code>&lt;style&gt;</code>
content model so the <code>&lt;img onerror&gt;</code> breaks out as live HTML.</p>
<p>Empirically (Chrome 150, verified with the probe pages in <code>test/</code>), the
shipping engine does <em>not</em> expand patches after sanitization: on the string
path the expansion fires during <code>sanitize()</code>'s own parse, so the walk sees and
sanitizes the fully materialized tree, teleports into MathML/SVG integration
points included; on the <code>IN_PLACE</code> path it fires on connection, before the
walk. So DOMPurify is not blind to the patch today. The attribute-layer removal
is therefore defense-in-depth rather than the sole barrier: <code>for</code> is kept only
on <code>&lt;label&gt;</code>/<code>&lt;output&gt;</code> and dropped everywhere else (notably <code>&lt;template for&gt;</code>),
<code>patchsrc</code> is dropped outright, both gated on <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code> (default-on), and
processing-instruction range markers are removed by <code>_isUnsafeNode</code>. On the
<code>IN_PLACE</code> path the same linkage is additionally severed across the whole tree
in a pre-pass before the walk begins
(<a href="#12-cross-realm-dom-input-and-in_place">§12</a>). That way live linkage cannot
survive into the output and re-expand in the caller's context, and behaviour
stays deterministic if a future engine defers expansion. Turning <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code>
off, or re-adding these attributes from a hook that runs <em>after</em> validation,
re-opens the vector - and, per the selectedcontent lesson, re-verify against the
shipping engine on each release.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> treat post-sanitize <em>content relocation</em> the same as
post-sanitize <em>content cloning</em> - the attributes that wire an element to a
deferred DOM mutation (<code>for</code> off <code>&lt;label&gt;</code>/<code>&lt;output&gt;</code>, <code>patchsrc</code>, PI range
markers) must not survive sanitization, because the sanitizer cannot follow
where the content lands.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">20. Server-side DOMs: jsdom is part of the TCB</h2><a id="user-content-20-server-side-doms-jsdom-is-part-of-the-tcb" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 20. Server-side DOMs: jsdom is part of the TCB" href="#20-server-side-doms-jsdom-is-part-of-the-tcb"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>When DOMPurify runs server-side, the DOM implementation is part of the trusted
computing base. A server-side sanitizer is only as accurate as the DOM it uses;
bugs or parser differences in jsdom or any alternative DOM can become sanitizer
bypasses even when the sanitizer's own logic is correct.</p>
<p>This matters because server-side parsing can differ from browser parsing.
<code>noscript</code> is a common example: with scripting disabled (the usual server-side
case), its contents parse as HTML rather than inert text. Form-based DOM
clobbering of built-ins is the opposite case - it reproduces in browsers but
<strong>not</strong> under jsdom (<a href="#11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups">§11</a>).</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="Bad assumption:  &quot;It passed in Chrome, so the server-side sanitizer is safe.&quot;
Better:          &quot;Test the exact DOM implementation you deploy.&quot;"><pre lang="text" class="notranslate"><code>Bad assumption:  "It passed in Chrome, so the server-side sanitizer is safe."
Better:          "Test the exact DOM implementation you deploy."
</code></pre></div>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> test the same DOM implementation you deploy. Browser
tests do not automatically prove jsdom safety, and jsdom tests do not
automatically prove browser safety.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">21. Configuration foot-guns checklist</h2><a id="user-content-21-configuration-foot-guns-checklist" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 21. Configuration foot-guns checklist" href="#21-configuration-foot-guns-checklist"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Before accepting a non-default configuration, require a reason for every one of
these:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="SAFE_FOR_XML: false
SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES: true
ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS: true
ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR: [...]
ADD_DATA_URI_TAGS: [...]
ALLOWED_URI_REGEXP: /.../
ADD_TAGS: [...]            // or () =&gt; ...
ADD_ATTR: [...]            // or () =&gt; ...
CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING: {...}
SANITIZE_DOM: false
SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS: false
WHOLE_DOCUMENT: true
RETURN_DOM: true
RETURN_DOM_FRAGMENT: true
IN_PLACE: true
NAMESPACE: '...'
PARSER_MEDIA_TYPE: '...'"><pre>SAFE_FOR_XML: <span class="pl-c1">false</span>
SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PROTOCOLS: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
ADD_URI_SAFE_ATTR: <span class="pl-kos">[</span>...<span class="pl-kos">]</span>
ADD_DATA_URI_TAGS: <span class="pl-kos">[</span>...<span class="pl-kos">]</span>
ALLOWED_URI_REGEXP: <span class="pl-pds"><span class="pl-c1">/</span>...<span class="pl-c1">/</span></span>
ADD_TAGS: <span class="pl-kos">[</span>...<span class="pl-kos">]</span>            <span class="pl-c">// or () =&gt; ...</span>
ADD_ATTR: <span class="pl-kos">[</span>...<span class="pl-kos">]</span>            <span class="pl-c">// or () =&gt; ...</span>
CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING: <span class="pl-kos">{</span>...<span class="pl-kos">}</span>
SANITIZE_DOM: <span class="pl-c1">false</span>
SANITIZE_NAMED_PROPS: <span class="pl-c1">false</span>
WHOLE_DOCUMENT: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
RETURN_DOM: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
RETURN_DOM_FRAGMENT: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
IN_PLACE: <span class="pl-c1">true</span>
NAMESPACE: <span class="pl-s">'...'</span>
PARSER_MEDIA_TYPE: <span class="pl-s">'...'</span></pre></div>
<p>General review questions:</p>
<ol>
<li>Does this flag widen the tag allow-list?</li>
<li>Does it widen the attribute allow-list?</li>
<li>Does it affect URI validation?</li>
<li>Does it change the output type?</li>
<li>Does it change the parsing namespace or media type?</li>
<li>Does it disable DOM clobbering protection?</li>
<li>Does it make sanitized output flow into another interpreter?</li>
<li>Does it rely on a framework-specific assumption?</li>
<li>Does it disable lexical guards that compensate for DOM parser information loss?</li>
<li>Does it allow an element the engine mutates, clones, or teleports <em>after</em> sanitization (e.g. <code>template</code> + declarative partial updates, <code>selectedcontent</code>)?</li>
<li>Does it pass DOM-node input from a different document context (XML/XHTML parse, <code>importNode</code>, <code>setAttributeNS</code>) or a live, already-connected tree?</li>
<li>Does it rely on an <code>afterSanitize*</code> hook as a security policy, or on a hook that detaches nodes?</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Rule:</strong> secure defaults are the product. Configuration is where many
application-specific bypasses are born.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">22. Mixed document contexts: DOM-node input from XML/XHTML and the DOM API</h2><a id="user-content-22-mixed-document-contexts-dom-node-input-from-xmlxhtml-and-the-dom-api" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 22. Mixed document contexts: DOM-node input from XML/XHTML and the DOM API" href="#22-mixed-document-contexts-dom-node-input-from-xmlxhtml-and-the-dom-api"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p><code>sanitize()</code> accepts a DOM node, not only a string. On the string path DOMPurify
controls the parser, so every tree it walks is one the HTML parser can build.
On the node path the caller controls the tree, and two families of trees exist
that the HTML parser can <em>never</em> produce but the DOM API and an XML/XHTML parse
can. Both were closed in 3.4.14 and 3.4.15 (proactive hardening, no advisory).</p>
<p><strong>Case-preserved attribute names.</strong> The HTML parser ASCII-lowercases attribute
names, so <code>ONERROR</code> in a string arrives as <code>onerror</code>. <code>setAttributeNS(null, 'ONERROR', …)</code> does not lowercase, and neither does an XHTML parse imported via
<code>importNode()</code>. DOMPurify's policy check lowercases the name and correctly
rejects it - but the removal was name-based, and <code>removeAttribute('ONERROR')</code>
lowercases only its <em>lookup key</em>, never the stored qualified name, so it found
nothing and the attribute survived. Reinserted via <code>innerHTML</code> it lowercases
into a live handler.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const img = document.createElement('img');
img.setAttribute('src', 'x');
img.setAttributeNS(null, 'ONERROR', 'alert(1)'); // no XML needed
DOMPurify.sanitize(img.parentNode || img);

// or:
new DOMParser().parseFromString(
  '&lt;img xmlns=&quot;http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml&quot; src=&quot;x&quot; ONERROR=&quot;alert(1)&quot;/&gt;',
  'application/xhtml+xml'
).documentElement;"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">img</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'img'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">img</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">setAttribute</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'src'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s">'x'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">img</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">setAttributeNS</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">null</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s">'ONERROR'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s">'alert(1)'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span> <span class="pl-c">// no XML needed</span>
<span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">img</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">parentNode</span> <span class="pl-c1">||</span> <span class="pl-s1">img</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-c">// or:</span>
<span class="pl-k">new</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMParser</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">parseFromString</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span>
  <span class="pl-s">'&lt;img xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml" src="x" ONERROR="alert(1)"/&gt;'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-s">'application/xhtml+xml'</span>
<span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">documentElement</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Fix: remove the exact <code>Attr</code> node (<code>removeAttributeNode</code>, which is case- and
namespace-exact) through the cached <code>Element.prototype</code> reference, and treat a
form that shadows <code>removeAttributeNode</code>/<code>getAttributeNode</code> as clobbered
(<a href="#11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups">§11</a>).</p>
<p><strong>Literal-text elements with impossible children.</strong> The fragment serializer
emits the text children of <code>style</code>, <code>script</code>, <code>xmp</code>, <code>iframe</code>, <code>noembed</code>,
<code>noframes</code>, <code>plaintext</code> and <code>noscript</code> unescaped. Two node-built shapes ride on
that:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="// (a) an element child as a &quot;shield&quot;, then a text sibling carrying the end tag
const el = document.createElement('style');
el.appendChild(document.createElement('b'));
el.appendChild(document.createTextNode('&lt;/style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;'));

// (b) text-only content that already carries the element's OWN end tag
const el2 = document.createElement('xmp');
el2.appendChild(document.createTextNode('&lt;/xmp&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;'));"><pre><span class="pl-c">// (a) an element child as a "shield", then a text sibling carrying the end tag</span>
<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">el</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'style'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">el</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">appendChild</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'b'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">el</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">appendChild</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createTextNode</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'&lt;/style&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-c">// (b) text-only content that already carries the element's OWN end tag</span>
<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">el2</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'xmp'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">el2</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">appendChild</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createTextNode</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'&lt;/xmp&gt;&lt;img src=x onerror=alert(1)&gt;'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Shape (a) slipped the generic markup probe because that probe self-disables
once there is an element child, and the dedicated guard covered <code>style</code> only.
Shape (b) is caught on the HTML path by the <code>innerHTML</code>-based probes, but an
XML/XHTML working document serializes <code>innerHTML</code> with <code>&lt;</code> escaped, which
silently blinds them. Both shapes are now checked against <code>textContent</code> (the
raw-serialized form for these elements), for the whole literal-text set, with a
per-element end-tag matcher: on reparse only an element's <em>own</em> end tag
terminates its raw content, and the lookahead requires a tag-name terminator so
<code>&lt;/styles</code> is not mistaken for <code>&lt;/style</code>. <code>style</code> is allowed by default, so
shape (a) needed no configuration at all; the others need the tag allow-listed.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> DOM-node input must be sanitized for the tree it <em>is</em>,
not for the tree an HTML parse of its serialization would have produced. Remove
attributes by node, not by name; treat literal-text elements whose serialization
can re-open markup as unsafe; and never let the probe depend on a serialization
(<code>innerHTML</code>) whose escaping differs between document types.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">23. Hook lifecycle: detached nodes, kept custom elements, and allow-list leaks</h2><a id="user-content-23-hook-lifecycle-detached-nodes-kept-custom-elements-and-allow-list-leaks" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: 23. Hook lifecycle: detached nodes, kept custom elements, and allow-list leaks" href="#23-hook-lifecycle-detached-nodes-kept-custom-elements-and-allow-list-leaks"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Hooks are part of the trust boundary: a hook that runs at the wrong point, or
not at all, turns an application's own security policy into a gap.</p>
<p><strong>Hook-detached nodes.</strong> A long-standing user pattern removes the current node
from inside <code>beforeSanitizeElements</code> / <code>uponSanitizeElement</code> (issue #469,
draw.io-style <code>foreignObject</code> filtering). Two hazards followed. First, the
subsequent unsafe-node and namespace checks would call <code>_forceRemove</code> on a
parentless node and hit the fail-closed throw that exists for nodes DOMPurify
wants gone but <em>cannot</em> detach - the opposite case. Since 3.4.12 a node that is
not the walk root and has no parent (read through the cached getter) is
treated as removed and skipped. Second, on the <code>IN_PLACE</code> path a hook-detached
subtree is not in <code>DOMPurify.removed</code>, so the post-walk neutralization pass
never reached it, yet an <code>&lt;img onload&gt;</code> that began loading when the caller
built the live tree still fires in page scope after <code>sanitize()</code> returns. Since
3.4.13 the detached subtree is neutralized inline at the point of detection.</p>
<p><strong>Kept custom elements skipped <code>afterSanitizeElements</code>.</strong> An element admitted by
<code>CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING.tagNameCheck</code> exited the element pipeline early and
never reached <code>afterSanitizeElements</code>. An application using that hook as a
policy layer ("strip attribute X from every surviving element") saw X removed
from normal elements and preserved on custom elements; when the custom element
later re-injects the value into an HTML sink, that is a second-order XSS gadget
(<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-c2j3-45gr-mqc4">GHSA-c2j3-45gr-mqc4</a>,
reported by @Rikuxx0, fixed in 3.4.12). The hook now runs for kept custom
elements exactly as for allow-listed ones; the deliberately skipped namespace
and fallback-tag <em>removal</em> checks stay skipped, since they are removal
decisions, not the hook contract.</p>
<p><strong>Allow-list mutation leaking across calls.</strong> <code>uponSanitize*</code> hooks receive
<code>allowedTags</code> / <code>allowedAttributes</code>. Writing to those objects mutated a shared
binding - first the per-instance defaults
(<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-76mc-f452-cxcm">GHSA-76mc-f452-cxcm</a>,
fixed in 3.4.7), then the binding captured by <code>setConfig()</code>, which the 3.4.7
clone-guard did not cover
(<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories/GHSA-cmwh-pvxp-8882">GHSA-cmwh-pvxp-8882</a>,
fixed in 3.4.11), then a timing gap where a hook installed lazily from inside
<code>beforeSanitizeElements</code> was seen after the pre-walk guard had already sampled
the hook arrays (fixed in 3.4.13). The allow-list is now forked off any shared
binding immediately before it is exposed to a hook, idempotently per call.</p>
<p><strong>Defensive invariant:</strong> every surviving element takes the same hook path;
nodes a hook detaches are treated as removed <em>and</em> neutralized when live; and
nothing a hook can reach is shared across calls.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">How to test sanitizer safety</h2><a id="user-content-how-to-test-sanitizer-safety" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: How to test sanitizer safety" href="#how-to-test-sanitizer-safety"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>For each payload class, test behavior through the actual sinks your product uses.</p>
<p>Minimum harness:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="function assertNoActiveContent(root) {
  console.assert(!root.querySelector('script'));
  console.assert(!root.querySelector('[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'));
  console.assert(!root.querySelector('a[href^=&quot;javascript:&quot; i]'));
  console.assert(!root.querySelector('iframe, object, embed'));
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">function</span> <span class="pl-en">assertNoActiveContent</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">root</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">root</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'script'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">root</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'[onerror],[onload],[onclick],[onfocus]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">root</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'a[href^="javascript:" i]'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">assert</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-c1">!</span><span class="pl-s1">root</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">querySelector</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'iframe, object, embed'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p>String-output test:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty);

const container = document.createElement('div');
container.innerHTML = clean;

assertNoActiveContent(container);"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">container</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-en">assertNoActiveContent</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">container</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span></pre></div>
<p>Multi-sink test:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="const clean = DOMPurify.sanitize(dirty);

const sinks = [
  html =&gt; {
    const d = document.createElement('div');
    d.innerHTML = html;
    return d;
  },

  html =&gt; {
    const t = document.createElement('template');
    t.innerHTML = html;
    return t.content;
  },

  html =&gt; {
    const iframe = document.createElement('iframe');
    document.body.appendChild(iframe);
    iframe.contentDocument.write(html);
    return iframe.contentDocument;
  }
];

for (const sink of sinks) {
  const dom = sink(clean);
  assertNoActiveContent(dom);
}"><pre><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">clean</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">DOMPurify</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">sanitize</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dirty</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">sinks</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-kos">[</span>
  <span class="pl-s1">html</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">d</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'div'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">d</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">html</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">d</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>

  <span class="pl-s1">html</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">t</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'template'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">t</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">innerHTML</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">html</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">t</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">content</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>

  <span class="pl-s1">html</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">iframe</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">createElement</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s">'iframe'</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-smi">document</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">body</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">appendChild</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">iframe</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">iframe</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">contentDocument</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">write</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">html</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">iframe</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">contentDocument</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
<span class="pl-kos">]</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>

<span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">sink</span> <span class="pl-k">of</span> <span class="pl-s1">sinks</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">dom</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">sink</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">clean</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
  <span class="pl-en">assertNoActiveContent</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">dom</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">;</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span></pre></div>
<p>Test these invariants:</p>
<ol>
<li>
<strong>No executable attributes</strong> after insertion.</li>
<li>
<strong>No unsafe URLs</strong> after URL normalization.</li>
<li>
<strong>No disallowed tags</strong> after parser repair.</li>
<li>
<strong>No namespace surprise</strong> after serialization and reparsing.</li>
<li>
<strong>No template expressions</strong> after text-node merging.</li>
<li>
<strong>No clobbering names</strong> when named-property isolation is expected (test
in-browser - jsdom cannot reproduce form clobbering, <a href="#11-dom-clobbering-markup-changes-object-lookups">§11</a>/<a href="#20-server-side-doms-jsdom-is-part-of-the-tcb">§20</a>).</li>
<li>
<strong>No prototype-pollution downgrade</strong> when <code>Object.prototype</code> is polluted.</li>
<li>
<strong>No deferred clone resurrection</strong> after engine features settle.</li>
<li>
<strong>No depth-limit flattening bypass</strong> at implementation thresholds such as 512
and at deliberately oversized inputs such as 8192 nested nodes.</li>
<li>
<strong>No rawtext/RCDATA attribute breakout</strong> through values containing
parser-control syntax.</li>
<li>
<strong>No timeout or stack blow-up</strong> on adversarial nesting, including
<code>KEEP_CONTENT</code> hoisting of nested disallowed elements.</li>
<li>
<strong>No case-preserved attribute survival</strong> on DOM-node input built with
<code>setAttributeNS</code> or imported from an XHTML parse
(<a href="#22-mixed-document-contexts-dom-node-input-from-xmlxhtml-and-the-dom-api">§22</a>).</li>
<li>
<strong>No literal-text breakout</strong> from a node-built <code>style</code>/<code>xmp</code>/<code>iframe</code>/… that
carries an element child or its own end tag.</li>
<li>
<strong>Uniform hook lifecycle</strong>: <code>afterSanitizeElements</code> fires for kept custom
elements; a node detached by a hook is gone from the output and, in
<code>IN_PLACE</code>, from the live tree's armed state
(<a href="#23-hook-lifecycle-detached-nodes-kept-custom-elements-and-allow-list-leaks">§23</a>).</li>
<li>
<strong>Fail closed on <code>IN_PLACE</code> throws</strong>: a forbidden or clobbered root, or an
exception mid-walk, must leave no non-allow-listed attribute in the live
subtree (<a href="#12-cross-realm-dom-input-and-in_place">§12</a>).</li>
</ol>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Status: known bypasses against the current release</h2><a id="user-content-status-known-bypasses-against-the-current-release" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Status: known bypasses against the current release" href="#status-known-bypasses-against-the-current-release"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>As of DOMPurify 3.4.15, every attack class catalogued on this page has a
corresponding defense in the shipped code, and the representative vectors for
each class are neutralized. This was checked empirically, not asserted from
memory: the vectors below (and the prototype-pollution variants, run with
<code>Object.prototype</code> actively polluted) were executed against <code>main</code>, and each
output was re-parsed and inspected for <em>live</em> dangerous nodes - executable
<code>&lt;script&gt;</code>, <code>on*</code> event-handler attributes, and <code>javascript:</code> URLs in
navigable attributes - rather than matched as strings. None survived.</p>
<p>The classes and their current defenses:</p>
<ul>
<li>Mutation XSS, namespace confusion, rawtext/RCDATA breakouts, foster
parenting, and integration-point re-entry (sections 2-7, 10) are closed by
the namespace-validity check, the <code>SAFE_FOR_XML</code> markup probes (including the
rawtext-element coverage added for CVE-2026-0540), and the depth handling.</li>
<li>DOM clobbering (section 11) is closed by the realm-safe, cached-getter
clobbering probe.</li>
<li>Prototype pollution as sanitizer downgrade (section 15) is closed by
building the internal configuration objects prototype-free
(<code>Object.create(null)</code>) and cloning incoming config before use, so a polluted
<code>Object.prototype</code> cannot inject permissive checks. This was the root of
CVE-2024-45801 (the <code>__depth</code> weakening) and CVE-2026-41238 (the
<code>CUSTOM_ELEMENT_HANDLING</code> fallback); both were verified fixed at the
mechanism level, not just by output.</li>
<li>Template-expression reassembly (section 13) is closed by the corrected
template-literal expressions (the CVE-2025-26791 regression) applied across
every DOM-returning path.</li>
<li>URI scheme confusion (section 17) is closed by the allow-list URI test after
whitespace and control-character stripping.</li>
<li>Engine-deferred mutation via <code>selectedcontent</code> (section 18, CVE-2026-47423)
is closed by forbidding the element unless explicitly opted in; declarative
partial updates (section 19) are closed by stripping the patch linkage in the
attribute layer and, for <code>IN_PLACE</code>, in a pre-walk sweep.</li>
<li>Cross-realm and <code>IN_PLACE</code> input (section 12) is closed by the fail-closed
neutralize-before-throw contract, the removal-record bookkeeping fix, and the
fragment shadow-root traversal fix (3.4.12-3.4.15).</li>
<li>Mixed document contexts on DOM-node input (section 22) are closed by
exact-<code>Attr</code>-node removal through cached prototype references and the
<code>textContent</code>-based literal-text guard over the full literal-text element set
(3.4.14, 3.4.15).</li>
<li>Hook-lifecycle gaps (section 23) are closed by treating hook-detached nodes as
removed and neutralizing them when live, running <code>afterSanitizeElements</code> for
kept custom elements (GHSA-c2j3-45gr-mqc4), and forking hook-visible
allow-lists off every shared binding (GHSA-76mc-f452-cxcm,
GHSA-cmwh-pvxp-8882).</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Important caveat on how this was verified.</strong> The empirical run used jsdom
(the regression tests for sections 22 and 23 build their trees with the DOM API
precisely so they run identically under jsdom and the three browser engines;
the shadow-root and form-clobbering cases are browser-only).
jsdom's parser is not a browser parser, and several of these classes -
mutation XSS and namespace confusion above all - depend on real-browser parser
behavior that jsdom does not reproduce. A clean jsdom result is necessary but
not sufficient. The authoritative confirmation for the parser-dependent classes
is the project's Playwright matrix across Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit (and the
legacy-engine snapshots), which exercises these vectors in real engines. Treat
the jsdom run documented here as the reproducible lower bound and the browser
matrix as the real oracle.</p>
<p><strong>What "no known bypass" does and does not mean.</strong> It means the documented
classes are closed against their known representatives in a supported release.
It does not mean the search is over: new browser features (the
<code>selectedcontent</code> and customizable-select work is a live example), new parser
quirks, and new foreign-content rules keep arriving, and the next bypass class
is usually one of those boundaries moving. The defensive invariants in the
sections above, not a fixed payload list, are the durable regression target. If
you find a working bypass against a supported release, report it privately
through the project's
<a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify/security/advisories">security advisories</a>.</p>
<hr>
<div class="markdown-heading"><h2 class="heading-element">Final lesson</h2><a id="user-content-final-lesson" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Final lesson" href="#final-lesson"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p>Most sanitizer bypasses are not "forgot to remove <code>&lt;script&gt;</code>". They happen at
boundaries:</p>
<ul>
<li>between one parser and another;</li>
<li>between HTML, SVG, MathML, XML, and template syntax;</li>
<li>between string output and DOM output;</li>
<li>between default config and application-specific config;</li>
<li>between a clean JavaScript realm and a polluted one;</li>
<li>between the tree the sanitizer walked and the tree the browser later mutates;</li>
<li>between what the DOM can represent and what the source string made the parser
do.</li>
</ul>
<p>That is the real regression target: not a list of scary tags, but a set of
parser, DOM, configuration, lexical-guard, and runtime invariants that must
remain true across browsers and across time.</p>

              </div>

          </div>
</div>
